# Code review 2026-10-13 — T-056 bounded navigation parser

- **Change:** development, cumulative fd9009bb510a8351cb3f8f404f2db9d76c978134..830de9d075aeabe5461b7aca204acfb5fc1507ba; 357 frozen paths, including exact original evidence.
- **Reviewer:** independent R1 and R2 contexts and explicitly attributed replacement R2 fix reviewers; coordinator consolidation. No maintainer approval is attributed.
- **Date:** first review2026-10-13; final committed-fix verification and canonical filing2026-10-15.
- **Risk class:** Ordinary host-only tooling under the standard subsystem definition; the maintainer-requested separate security pass covers documentation integrity/availability. No kernel, userspace, unsafe, dependency or CI configuration change.
- **Security-review cross-reference:** [paired security record](../security-reviews/2026-10-13-t056-parser.md).
- **Authority:** [ADR-0043](../../../decisions/0043-documentation-corpus-consistency.md), [approved plan](../../reports/2026-10-12-phase-b2-track2-preflight-and-plan.md), [parser preflight](../../reports/2026-10-13-t056-parser-preflight.md), [T-056](../../tasks/phase-b2/T-056-docs-consistency-automation.md).

## Correctness

The command checks public local navigation with explicit source offsets, UTF-8, physical lines, bounded Markdown containers/tables, original inline-token boundaries and heading/HTML anchors. Invalid or unsupported relevant input produces sorted diagnostics. Tests-first corrections address actual false greens, false reds and resource regressions; failed drafts and corrected test oracles are separately retained. Current per-closer failed-search bounds preserve future eligible openers and partial consumption. Raw DEL is excluded from URI autolinks without hiding ordinary local Markdown links.

## Style

Python 3.9+ standard library, English source, documented contracts and source-aware shared consumers follow the approved policy. Syntax compilation is recorded as compilation, with no invented Python formatter/linter. Exact verbose failure streams retain their original trailing spaces; the source/Markdown whitespace check passes separately from the disclosed raw-evidence warnings.

## Test coverage

The final author and each current independent fix reviewer execute all 149 fixture methods on Python 3.9.6 and 3.13.14, 842 attributed complete report/status/stderr comparisons and four whole-public-corpus/default-root/cwd runs. Focused controls cover URI controls, protected inline syntax, delimiter classes/partial matches, authority/error paths and finite large-input observations. Counts belong to their respective executions; overlapping sets are not summed as unique tests. Limited primary-source comparisons do not establish renderer conformance or a universal CPU/memory bound.

## Documentation

The CI guide states supported grammar, source/path ownership, limits, usage and the Step 11-only boundary. The T-049-owned quoted-reference repair preserves historical words and verdicts. Existing author verification/correction records and all original raw evidence retain their identities. This consolidation records actual review chronology, withdrawals, replacement contexts and remaining assurance limits instead of rewriting earlier reports.

## Integration

Script-relative default root and Git-free isolated fixtures work from both repository and outside cwd. The final frozen corpus is 387 Markdown files, 6764 local targets and 786 fragments with zero findings; later review-publication navigation is a separate coordinator capture. CI wiring and metadata families belong to Step 12. T-056 remains In Progress with all sixteen full-task criteria unchecked. No hosted, runtime, unsafe-soundness, Done or publication claim is made.

## Verdict

**Approve** — both required review rounds and actual committed-fix verification complete for the selected Step 11 parser scope; zero unresolved verified source findings. Full T-056 delivery remains pending Step 12/13 and hosted evidence.

# Code review 2026-10-13 — T-056 Step 11 parser, R1 INITIAL

- **Change:** clean `development`, `fd9009bb510a8351cb3f8f404f2db9d76c978134..ca1f0735bed114228a4b8a165162e27fbacd67b3`; 20 paths, 2,175 insertions and 12 deletions.
- **Reviewer:** independent Codex R1 CODE agent, supplied dispatch `gpt-6.1-sol / high / fork_turns:none`; this identifies the dispatch, not model introspection or maintainer approval.
- **Date:** 2026-10-13.
- **Risk class:** Ordinary host-only tooling under the standard's subsystem definition; filesystem/input-boundary risks assessed here. The approved execution additionally requires paired independent code/security rounds.
- **Security-review cross-reference:** pending the actual independent R1 security INITIAL. No current Step 11 peer report, finding or verdict was read or supplied before this checkpoint. This artifact grants no paired approval.
- **Canonical destination:** `docs/analysis/reviews/code-reviews/2026-10-13-t056-parser.md`. Relative links below resolve from that directory. The root coordinator owns repository publication/indexing; this reviewer writes only its `/tmp/tyrne-b22-step11-r1-code*` artifacts.
- **Authority:** [approved plan](../../reports/2026-10-12-phase-b2-track2-preflight-and-plan.md), [T-056](../../tasks/phase-b2/T-056-docs-consistency-automation.md), [parser preflight](../../reports/2026-10-13-t056-parser-preflight.md), [implementation/evidence](../../reports/2026-10-13-t056-parser-implementation.md), [ADR-0043](../../../decisions/0043-documentation-corpus-consistency.md), [code standard](../../../standards/code-review.md), [perform-code-review skill](../../../../.agents/skills/perform-code-review/SKILL.md), [conduct-review skill](../../../../.agents/skills/conduct-review/SKILL.md), and [code master plan](master-plan.md).

## Correctness

The existing forty fixtures and actual public corpus pass independently. Adversarial inputs expose eight blocking contract/boundary defects, listed below, and one performance advisory. A green current corpus does not establish that the documented grammar or invalid-input boundary is correct. The five master-plan roles were performed sequentially by this independent reviewer; they are not five separate reviewers.

- **C01 — Medium, blocker: initial input-directory symlinks bypass the read boundary.** `tools/docs_consistency.py:564–568` passes `root/docs` and `root/.agents/skills` directly to `os.walk`. The nested-directory symlink test at line 575 does not inspect the starting directory or intermediate `.agents` component. `load` at line 633 checks only the final Markdown leaf. In the isolated `symlink_root` fixture, `docs` points to a harmless reviewer-owned sibling outside the fixture root containing `exposed.md`; the scanner reads it and returns `ok: true, files: 1`, with no finding. This violates the documented symlink-input prohibition and permits reading beyond the selected root before link resolution. No real private content was used. **Action:** validate every input path component and resolved containment before enumeration/opening; reject symlinked starts/intermediate components with a deterministic nonzero input finding. Add an outside-root sentinel fixture that proves its contents are never read, including `.agents/skills` start/intermediate aliases.

- **C02 — Medium, blocker: Markdown title/destination syntax is reparsed as HTML or comments.** `tools/docs_consistency.py:354, 362, 448–455` masks comments before parsing Markdown titles, and skips destination/title spans in the inline cursor without erasing them from the HTML-visible stream. Fixture `[x](target.md "<a href='missing.md'>")` produces a false missing-target finding at `docs/source.md:1`. More seriously, `[x](target.md "<span id='fake'>")` followed by `[y](#fake)` returns green with a fragment count of one, accepting an anchor that exists only as title text. A title containing `<!-- literal` also causes an incorrect unclosed-title diagnostic and loses subsequent real links. **Action:** establish lexical precedence for link destinations/titles before HTML/comment extraction, preserve source offsets, and expose only actual HTML tokens to `Anchors`. Add the false-positive, false-green and comment-in-title regressions together; keep real HTML href/id behavior covered.

- **C03 — Low, blocker: advertised reference continuations fail inside blockquotes.** `tools/docs_consistency.py:412–421` reads continuation lines from raw source without removing their quote container. The fixture below yields an undefined explicit reference at line 1 and an unsupported continuation/title at line 3; the same definition on one quoted line works. Both quote containers and reference-definition continuations are advertised, with no exclusion for this composition. **Action:** parse container-aware continuation lines with original offsets and add positive/negative quoted continuation fixtures.

```text
> [x][id]
>
> [id]:
>   target.md
```

- **C04 — Low, blocker: list-marker padding turns indented code into an active link.** `tools/docs_consistency.py:66–69` consumes all whitespace following a list marker before `blocks` can recognize code indentation. The single line `-     [literal](missing.md)` yields a missing-target finding at line 1 even though the five spaces after the marker introduce an indented code block. This contradicts the guide's container/indented-code exclusion. **Action:** retain the indentation implied by excess list-marker padding, and test both this literal example and an ordinary list prose link that must remain active.

- **C05 — Low, blocker: escape parity misclassifies an image inside a link label.** `tools/docs_consistency.py:442` treats any backslash immediately before `!` as escaping the image marker, including the second character of an escaped backslash pair. The fixture below contains exactly two backslashes before `!`; it should contain an image inside the outer link, but produces the unsupported nested-link diagnostic at line 1. **Action:** derive image status from the lexer or preceding-backslash parity; add paired odd/even escape fixtures. Genuine nested links should still fail.

```text
[outer \\![image](target.md)](target.md)
```

- **C06 — Low, blocker: explicit empty angle reference destinations are rejected.** `tools/docs_consistency.py:414–415` conflates a parsed empty destination with an absent destination. `[x][id]`, a blank line, then `[id]: <>` yields an undefined reference at line 1 and “Reference definition requires a destination” at line 3; adding a valid title does not help. The guide advertises reference links and angle/empty destinations without restricting the latter to inline links. **Action:** distinguish a consumed explicit empty angle destination from missing syntax, resolve it as a same-file target, and test both empty-angle reference variants and actually missing destinations.

- **C07 — Medium, blocker: a nonregular Markdown input can hang before the size limit applies.** `tools/docs_consistency.py:550–553` opens any enumerated `.md` path with blocking binary I/O. An isolated FIFO at `fifo/docs/blocked.md`, with no writer, blocks the CLI; the reviewer killed the subprocess after two seconds. The 16 MiB read limit does not constrain `open` on a FIFO. **Action:** reject nonregular inputs before opening, including source-line fragment targets, and add a time-bounded negative special-file fixture where supported. Return an input/read finding rather than waiting for a writer. This is an invalid-input contract defect, not evidence that the current repository contains a FIFO.

- **C08 — Low, advisory: unmatched backtick runs cause repeated suffix scans.** `tools/docs_consistency.py:131–155, 460–465` rescans a run from successive cursor positions when no closing span exists; comment processing also calls this path. Reviewer measurements for prose followed by 1,000/4,000/12,000 unmatched ticks were about 0.0055/0.0256/0.1129 seconds in the isolated parser process. A 150,000-tick document, well below 16 MiB, exceeded a four-second subprocess deadline. **Action:** consume an unmatched delimiter run once or cache paragraph/run boundaries; consider a large adversarial fixture before CI wiring. These host-specific observations show poor scaling, not an infinite loop or a portable timing threshold. The current documented bound is bytes/nesting/labels, so this is advisory.

- **C09 — Low, blocker: extra basename exclusions silently narrow the declared public corpus.** `tools/docs_consistency.py:573` discards any directory called `.git`, `.venv` or `__pycache__` anywhere under the selected public prefixes. The contract identifies exactly three local-only analysis prefixes, and otherwise includes all public `docs/**/*.md` and skill Markdown. A fixture with `docs/visible.md` plus `docs/.venv/public.md` containing a broken link returns green with only one file counted. Generic ignore patterns are not proof that a Markdown source can never be intentionally committed there; the implementation already force-adds a scoped raw `.log` despite its generic ignore. **Action:** remove unapproved public-prefix basename exclusions or reconcile their exact ownership/scope under the approved policy with explicit negative coverage. Do not silently turn the exact three-prefix boundary into a general ignore rule. No currently tracked file was shown to be omitted by this additional rule.

Diagnostics from the reproduced grammar failures retain the source line described above. The existing API returns sorted deduplicated findings; ordinary path/fragment/UTF-8 failures are translated to findings. C01, C02 and C07 prevent accepting that boundary as generally correct. The implementation does not claim complete CommonMark rendering, and these findings are limited to its stated grammar and input contract.

## Style

The module/CLI/test split is clear and has no new external dependency. `Finding`/`Link`/`Report`, offset-preserving blanking, explicit UTF-8 and structured JSON fit the host-tool role. I inspected existing `tools/branch-protection.py:1–100` as a Python style reference, and reviewed all three new Python files in full. In-memory compilation and Python 3.9 AST parsing passed on actual Python 3.9.6; neither is called a formatter or linter. No package installation occurred.

The parser's successive text masks make precedence harder to audit than the short functions suggest; C02 is a concrete maintainability/correctness consequence. C03/C04/C05 show that container and escape state should be retained rather than inferred from isolated substrings. This is targeted repair guidance, not a request for an unrelated rewrite. No Rust formatting, clippy, `unsafe` annotation, safety-audit or naming change is introduced by these files.

## Test coverage

I read all 269 lines of `tools/test-check-docs.py` before the parser and CLI. Its forty tests exercise positive/negative navigation syntax, reference normalization/definitions, image/link combinations, code/comment/fence boundaries, headings/HTML anchors, exact case/decoding, source lines, invalid UTF-8/roots, deterministic output, default-root/cwd behavior and no-write observations. The suite passed independently twice: 40 tests in 0.417 seconds, then 40 in 0.352 seconds with `TMPDIR` explicitly under this reviewer's own namespace. Existing regression fixtures for the author's D1 block-crossing false positive remain present; the author's pre-fix development observations are not independent committed before/after evidence.

Missing meaningful regressions are C01's starting/intermediate directory symlinks; C02's title HTML/comment precedence including fabricated anchors; C03's quoted reference continuations; C04's excess list padding; C05's even/odd escape parity; C06's empty angle reference destination; C07's special files; and C09's intentionally public Markdown under generated-name directories. C08 merits a time-bounded scaling probe before CI activation. Positive paired controls were checked for actual HTML, code in link labels, nested-list fences, fence-container termination, one-line quoted definitions and brackets inside titles. These controls behaved as expected.

No new repository regression file, scratch branch, broken commit or unrelated Rust/QEMU/Miri test run was created. Host Markdown behavior does not alter the kernel/boot path, so a QEMU smoke would not test these changes. Every adversarial subprocess was bounded (two to four seconds); timeouts were observations, never counted as checker success.

## Integration

The delivered command uses only the Python 3.9 standard library and stdout. Absolute default-root execution from `/tmp` with an empty `PATH` passed, demonstrating no Git executable/cwd dependency. Full tracked-file hash comparison before/after reviewer executions found no mutation. The CLI disables imported bytecode output. Tests create transient isolated fixture roots; the first reviewer suite invocation used their default temporary locations, and the later run explicitly set `TMPDIR` to `/tmp/tyrne-b22-step11-r1-code-probes/suite-tmp`. This is disclosed rather than claiming the first invocation's temporary paths were prefix-controlled. No persistent repository write occurred.

The three local-only analysis prefixes are existing `.gitignore` ownership classifications, have no tracked input in the inspected inventory, and are not public historical archives. Treating them as outside the clean-checkout source corpus is defensible under Accepted ADR-0043 only with the matching fail-closed active-link/alias rule; the existing fixtures cover that rule, and no actual private contents were read here. This is not an automatic exemption for historical broken links. C01 exposes a different input alias bypass, and C09 questions the extra unnamed basename exclusions. All public historical archives remain required input.

No runtime, kernel/HAL/BSP/userland/test-HAL, `.github`, `.gitignore`, ADR or audit-log diff exists in the cumulative range. All six required contexts and source-app binding 15368 remain unchanged as versioned source; no live setting read-back/change or hosted execution was performed. Other task/review/ADR/ledger/fence/audit/live checks, infrastructure-standard completion and required existing-job CI wiring belong to Step 12. HIGH T-038, the C/D gate, maintainer approval, optional general phase-exit accounting and PR49 publication barrier remain intact. This review does not authorize publication or task completion.

The 20-path diff is large chiefly because it retains full source/hash inventories and actual evidence, together with one cohesive parser/fixtures/CLI implementation. It is not an unreviewed runtime expansion. Commit subjects/trailers in the three inspected step commits follow English conventional style and reference ADR-0040/0043; the implementation uses `feat(tooling)`. No new crate or add-dependency workflow applies. Future CI must exercise the new paths; it currently does not.

## Full code-standard checklist

Each complete literal prompt from `docs/standards/code-review.md` follows in a tagged text fence so its copied relative links remain literal. Dispositions apply to the parser portion and do not imply whole-task delivery.

### Prompt 1 — Correctness

```text
- **Correctness.** Does the code do what the description says? Are there off-by-one errors, uninitialized fields, missing error handling?
```

**Disposition:** C01–C07 and C09 are blocking mismatches with the described input/grammar contract. Existing whole-corpus success is independently reproduced but does not cover these adversarial cases. C08 is a scaling advisory.

### Prompt 2 — Capability semantics

```text
- **Capability semantics.** If the change touches a trust boundary, is the capability check in the right place? Can an unauthorized caller observe or affect state they should not?
```

**Disposition:** No kernel capability API changes. The analogous host filesystem authority boundary is assessed explicitly: C01 reads outside the fixture root through an initial-directory alias; C09 broadens exclusions beyond the named scope. No real private content was read.

### Prompt 3 — Fault containment

```text
- **Fault containment.** Can a userspace failure reach the kernel through this path? If so, is that intended?
```

**Disposition:** No userspace/kernel execution path changes, so kernel fault containment is not exercised by this tool. C07 shows a host subprocess can block on invalid input; C08 shows CPU scaling risk before planned CI integration.

### Prompt 4 — `unsafe` discipline

```text
- **`unsafe` discipline.** Any new `unsafe` must satisfy [unsafe-policy.md](unsafe-policy.md): `SAFETY:` comment, audit log entry, justification, reviewer signed off specifically on the `unsafe`.
```

**Disposition:** No new Rust or unsafe region. No SAFETY/audit entry or unsafe sign-off is required or claimed; the cumulative audit/source scope is unchanged.

### Prompt 5 — Error handling

```text
- **Error handling.** Every `Result` propagated correctly? No new `unwrap`/`expect` on hot paths? Error types converted at boundaries (see [error-handling.md](error-handling.md))?
```

**Disposition:** Python boundaries convert ordinary OSError/ValueError/UTF-8 failures to deterministic findings; invalid CLI arguments exit 2 and invalid roots exit 1. C07 bypasses this behavior by blocking before an exception. Rust Result/unwrap/expect hot-path rules have no new application.

### Prompt 6 — Tests

```text
- **Tests.** Do tests actually test the change? See [testing.md](testing.md) for what counts.
```

**Disposition:** Forty meaningful isolated tests pass independently, but the specific missing cases are C01–C07/C09 and the advisory C08 scaling probe. Every current implemented family is links; pending Step 12 families are not falsely credited.

### Prompt 7 — Documentation

```text
- **Documentation.** Every new public item documented? Every `unsafe fn` has a `# Safety` section? ADR references in commit messages?
```

**Disposition:** All three modules, guide changes, task contracts and retained evidence were read. Usage and portion boundaries are documented; overclaims exposed by the findings need repair. No new Rust public item or unsafe fn; ADR trailers verified.

### Prompt 8 — Architectural principles

```text
- **Architectural principles.** Does the change respect [architectural-principles.md](architectural-principles.md), especially P1 (no ambient authority), P2 (small TCB), P3 (drivers in userspace), P7 (no proprietary blobs)?
```

**Disposition:** P1–P12 are individually disposed below. Host filesystem authority/input handling requires C01/C07 repair; no new kernel TCB, driver placement or proprietary blob is introduced.

### Prompt 9 — Dependencies

```text
- **Dependencies.** Any new crate added? Has it been through the dependency-addition process in [infrastructure.md](infrastructure.md)?
```

**Disposition:** Only stdlib imports; no new crate/package or install. add-dependency is not applicable. Actual Python 3.9 compatibility was checked in memory.

### Prompt 10 — Naming and style

```text
- **Naming and style.** Conforms to [code-style.md](code-style.md)? Clippy would already have caught most of this; reviewer does not re-litigate.
```

**Disposition:** Existing Python conventions spot-checked and all new source read. No invented formatter/linter or clippy assertion. Targeted lexer-state clarity is warranted by actual bugs, without unrelated style bikeshedding.

### Prompt 11 — Commit message

```text
- **Commit message.** Conforms to [commit-style.md](commit-style.md)? Trailers correct?
```

**Disposition:** The inspected kickoff 9b62a06, T049 repair 0c62391 and implementation ca1f073 subjects/trailers are English conventional commits with relevant ADR references. Implementation tooling scope matches the task DoD. No commit was created by this reviewer.

## Architectural principles P1–P12

| Principle | Individual disposition |
|---|---|
| P1 — No ambient authority | No kernel authority expansion. The caller selects a local root; C01 violates its intended read boundary and C07 fails to constrain input type. Host-only status does not excuse either. |
| P2 — Smallest defensible trusted computing base | Python tooling is outside the kernel TCB; no kernel dependency/size delta. The parser must still fail reliably before it becomes a required CI command. |
| P3 — Drivers, filesystems, and network stacks live in userspace | No driver implementation, placement or hardware service change. |
| P4 — Capability checks at every trust boundary | No kernel capability-check changes. The analogous host read boundary must be checked at enumeration and opening, not only link resolution; C01/C07 require repair. No private-content access is authorized by a public-corpus root. |
| P5 — Rust-only for kernel code | Accepted ADR-0043 expressly selects Python 3.9 stdlib for host documentation tooling. No Rust runtime implementation is replaced. |
| P6 — HAL separation | No hardware-specific code, HAL escape or board knowledge introduced. |
| P7 — No proprietary binary blobs in the kernel | Changed paths are source/docs/text evidence; no proprietary binary blob or fetched artifact added. |
| P8 — Decisions are recorded as ADRs | Accepted ADR-0040/0043 and approved plan govern this portion. No ADR altered or new architectural decision asserted. Exact corpus exclusions must remain within that authority (C09). |

### Primary rules and source correction

I actually read the complete numeric-end rule and all27 legacy replacement entries. HTML preserves valid noncharacters and unmapped controls while replacing null/surrogates/out-of-range values; its listed C1 mappings are source-specific. [WHATWG numeric-reference end state](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/parsing.html#numeric-character-reference-end-state). CommonMark's selected numeric grammar denotes the Unicode scalar, with decimal/hex spelling limits and invalid/null replacement; applying HTML's extra C1 table to Markdown would validate a different value. This is the source-rule distinction I checked against the implementation and anchor controls. [CommonMark numeric references](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#entity-and-numeric-character-references).

`numeric_reference` at `tools/docs_consistency.py:351` strips leading zeros, bounds significant digits before conversion, preserves valid scalar values and applies the explicit table only with `html=True`. `attribute_value` at line570 uses that HTML branch, while Markdown retains its own exact spelling/escape grammar. I independently transcribed and compared the entire27-entry WHATWG table, then exercised all32 C1 values through actual anchor resolution, including the five unmapped controls and wrong cross-source spellings.

The compatibility issue was inside the stdlib callback path: HTMLParser converts attribute references before calling the handler. [Python3.13 HTMLParser callback](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/html.parser.html#html.parser.HTMLParser.handle_starttag). Decimal conversion can fail at Python's integer-string limit; no process-wide setting change is an acceptable substitute for handling the supported input. [Python integer conversion limit](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/stdtypes.html#integer-string-conversion-length-limitation). At lines635–643/685, same-length ampersand masking prevents implicit conversion; public tag position/length recover the original prose spelling before the existing raw-attribute decoder. I inspected preservation of physical offsets and tested long tags with a later physical-line error on both interpreters. This makes no claim to override every HTMLParser behavior or prove global time/memory bounds.

## Correctness

### R2-C01 — Major, blocking: multiline HTML attribute content escapes into block, definition and heading parsing

**Locations:** [parser](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py), lines 136–190 (`blocks`, especially 174–178), 677–684 (pass order), 698–760 (`_definitions`), 813–817 (`_inlines` HTML-tag skip), and 860–884 (`_headings`). The supported contract in [CI guide](../../../guides/ci.md#documentation-consistency), line 147, explicitly says HTML attributes cannot create comments, links or anchors and unrelated prose remains checked.

`blocks(text)` processes each physical line before HTML attribute context is known. `_definitions` likewise sees attribute lines, and `_inlines` skips complete tags without removing their metadata from `heading_text`. Consequently, a valid multiline quoted HTML attribute can create an automatic anchor, consume a heading suffix, trigger a false definition diagnostic, or open a fake fence that hides subsequent real links. This is a tangible violation of the declared attribute-isolation contract; fixing it does not require a complete CommonMark renderer.

**Reproduction A:** place precisely this UTF-8 source in `README.md` of an otherwise empty temporary root, and run `python3 -B /absolute/path/to/tools/check-docs.py --root /temporary/root --json`:

```html
<div title="
# Ghost
">text</div>

[x](#ghost)
```

Actual on both Python 3.9.6 and 3.13.14: exit **0**, `files=1`, `local_targets=1`, `fragments=1`, empty findings. Expected: a missing-fragment finding at physical line 5; the document has no heading or explicit `ghost` anchor. Replacing the attribute with `title="normal"` correctly reports the missing fragment. Adding one real `# Ghost` after the HTML block incorrectly makes `#ghost-1` pass too, showing that the fake heading consumes an automatic suffix.

**Reproduction B:** replace the attribute's second line with three backticks, and put `[x](missing.md)` at physical line 5. Actual on both interpreters: exit **0**, `local_targets=0`, empty findings. Expected: a missing-target finding at line 5. A tilde-fence spelling followed by a real `<a href="missing.md">` has the same false-green result. A standalone `[ref]: missing.md` inside the attribute produces an erroneous `link-syntax` finding at line 2 instead of remaining literal metadata.

The `<div` example has unambiguous HTML-block context, ending at the blank line before the real link; quoted newlines are legal attribute content. This was checked against the [CommonMark 0.31.2 HTML-block start/end rules](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#html-blocks). Initial exploratory inline-title/span examples were not promoted to findings because their block interpretation was less clear.

**Required action:** add failing isolated regressions for multiline attribute ATX text, definition-looking text, backtick/tilde fences and following real links; isolate recognized tag/attribute content before it can alter block/definition/heading state while preserving raw relevant HTML attributes and exact physical offsets for actual anchors/links. Include controls for real headings, duplicate suffixes, actual `id`/`href`/`src`, quoted and unquoted attributes, code spans/fences and escaped HTML. Preserve visible failure for relevant unsupported syntax; do not suppress HTML or narrow the documented contract to get green. Re-run fixtures, both installed interpreters and the whole corpus.

**Evidence:** reviewer-owned `attribute-independent-probes.json` contains six cases on each interpreter; `attribute-block-probes.json` contains two additional cases on each. The setext exploratory case did report a fragment and is not claimed as a failure. No repository file was changed to reproduce the issue. Traceability: CR01/CR06/CR07; P8/P12; T056 AC1/AC2/AC9 and DoD1/DoD2.

Other independently inspected behavior: source-kind entity decoding and escape precedence, URL decoding exactly once, Unicode/control/noncharacter handling, attribute-context named references, exact-case/root/directory resolution, heading collision allocation, code/comment/container masking, explicit-reference diagnostics and physical source locations. The 60-case additional matrix passed outside the confirmed family. These observations do not turn the overall correctness pass green while R2-C01 remains.

## Eleven literal code-review checklist prompts

The following are the full literal prompts from [code-review standard](../../../standards/code-review.md), quoted as source text so their embedded relative links are not falsely presented as navigation from this review. Each receives its own disposition.

### CR01

```text
**Correctness.** Does the code do what the description says? Are there off-by-one errors, uninitialized fields, missing error handling?
```

**Blocking — R2-C01.** Valid multiline HTML metadata changes block/definition/heading behavior and permits false success. Other 60 independent matrix executions matched expectations; error/bounds handling was inspected, not assumed from the corpus run.

### CR02

```text
**Capability semantics.** If the change touches a trust boundary, is the capability check in the right place? Can an unauthorized caller observe or affect state they should not?
```

**Scoped pass; paired security pending.** No Tyrne capability ABI/state changes. Host authority is the selected root, and root/private/symlink/nonregular checks precede reads in the observed paths. Guarded outside-canary/FIFO test confirms those specific boundaries; no global race-proof claim.

### CR03

```text
**Fault containment.** Can a userspace failure reach the kernel through this path? If so, is that intended?
```

**Not applicable to kernel fault containment.** No userspace-to-kernel/runtime call path changed. Host failures remain CLI findings/nonzero exits; the parser correctness blocker remains separately recorded.

### CR04

```text
**`unsafe` discipline.** Any new `unsafe` must satisfy [unsafe-policy.md](unsafe-policy.md): `SAFETY:` comment, audit log entry, justification, reviewer signed off specifically on the `unsafe`.
```

**Not applicable, verified scope.** No Rust or new unsafe region is in the 50-path diff; no SAFETY/audit-log sign-off is invented. The historical T049 text remains evidence.

### CR05

```text
**Error handling.** Every `Result` propagated correctly? No new `unwrap`/`expect` on hot paths? Error types converted at boundaries (see [error-handling.md](error-handling.md))?
```

**Scoped pass for Python error boundaries.** Result/unwrap/expect prompts are Rust-specific. Python read/parse/resolve exceptions become categorized findings; CLI usage is 2, invalid root/UTF-8 are 1. Historical symlink-loop handling and numeric conversion bounds were inspected and tested.

### CR06

```text
**Tests.** Do tests actually test the change? See [testing.md](testing.md) for what counts.
```

**Blocking coverage gap — R2-C01.** Existing 62-method suite passes both runtimes and meaningfully catches historical regressions; independent reconstruction proves one four-subcase before/after failure. Add multiline attribute/block/heading interaction regressions and controls before fixing.

### CR07

```text
**Documentation.** Every new public item documented? Every `unsafe fn` has a `# Safety` section? ADR references in commit messages?
```

**Implementation-contract mismatch — R2-C01.** Guide/module contracts and workflow ownership are present; no new Rust public item or unsafe fn requires rustdoc. All six commit messages were read for ADR references; T049 evidence preservation was independently proven.

### CR08

```text
**Architectural principles.** Does the change respect [architectural-principles.md](architectural-principles.md), especially P1 (no ambient authority), P2 (small TCB), P3 (drivers in userspace), P7 (no proprietary blobs)?
```

**Individual P1–P12 dispositions below.** No runtime principle exception is introduced. P8/P12 require completion of the selected parser contract; R2-C01 blocks that scoped completion.

### CR09

```text
**Dependencies.** Any new crate added? Has it been through the dependency-addition process in [infrastructure.md](infrastructure.md)?
```

**Pass.** No crate or third-party Python package is added; imports are Python standard library. No dependency-addition process was bypassed or package installed.

### CR10

```text
**Naming and style.** Conforms to [code-style.md](code-style.md)? Clippy would already have caught most of this; reviewer does not re-litigate.
```

**Pass within host-Python scope.** Existing-tool comparison and full new-source read found no actively misleading naming/style blocker beyond the behavioral mask coordination issue. No unrelated clippy/rustfmt execution is claimed.

### CR11

```text
**Commit message.** Conforms to [commit-style.md](commit-style.md)? Trailers correct?
```

**Scoped pass; filing still pending.** Six English conventional commits and their ADR/task Refs were read. Canonical code/security review trailers/index filing follow actual paired review; no unseen final trailer or merge approval is claimed.

## Architectural principles — individual dispositions

| Principle | Disposition and evidence |
| --- | --- |
| P1 — No ambient authority | No runtime capability grant/ABI change. Host root selection is explicit and resolution refuses root/private escapes in reviewed paths; guarded canary test passes. No claim that Python is a capability sandbox. |
| P2 — Smallest defensible TCB | Host-only stdlib parser adds no privileged kernel code or build dependency. |
| P3 — Drivers/filesystems/network stacks in userspace | No placement/runtime/driver change. The delivered checker does not fetch external destinations. |
| P4 — Capability checks at trust boundaries | No syscall/IPC/cross-task entry changed. Host input boundaries inspected; actual paired security assessment remains pending. |
| P5 — Rust-only kernel code | No kernel/HAL/service implementation changed; Python host tooling is the approved ADR0043 choice. |
| P6 — HAL separation | No hardware/register/BSP dependency or kernel/HAL coupling added. |
| P7 — No proprietary blobs | Source and text evidence only; no proprietary binary or new external dependency. |

### Reconciliation of disclosed findings

**R2-SF01 — Medium, confirmed, attributed to the R2 security reviewer.** At [docs_consistency.py:1032](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1032), `urlsplit(value)` follows source-specific decoding and can change target identity. With only `foo.txt` present, `[x](< foo.txt>)` is missing on 3.9 but silently resolves the decoy on 3.13. With only the leading-space filename present, the outcome reverses. Entity-encoded leading space has the same discrepancy; percent-encoded space is a passing control. Current Python documents leading C0/space normalization and removal of ASCII tab/newline characters. I actually read the relevant documentation excerpts, rather than treating the helper as lossless. [Python URL parsing](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/urllib.parse.html#urllib.parse.urlsplit).

The blocker is source identity and advertised cross-version behavior, not a claim that all renderers preserve every control character. Browser HTML URL normalization must be handled as its own context; the peer's HTML tab/newline caveat is appropriate. Correction must give splitting an explicit origin-aware policy, preserve the declared target or visibly diagnose a deliberately unsupported spelling, and keep actual-versus-decoy controls across both supported interpreters. No target substitution or archive exclusion is acceptable.

**R2-SF02 — Medium, confirmed, attributed to the R2 security reviewer.** [Heading extraction at 862](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L862), [source-line bounds at 1067](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1067), related `splitlines()` consumers at 145/212/385/748 and LF-only offset indexes at 643/672 disagree on a physical line. For each of U+2028, U+2029, VT, FF, FS, GS, RS and NEL, `# one<separator># phantom` fabricates `#phantom`, and a two-piece source target fabricates `#L2`. Neither scalar is a physical line ending under the intended Markdown model. I actually read CommonMark's line-ending definition and reproduced LF/CR/CRLF positive controls. [CommonMark characters and lines](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#characters-and-lines).

Both interpreters also report line 1 for the missing link in `before\r\r[x](missing)`, whose physical line is 3. Correction needs a shared LF/CR/CRLF iterator and compatible original-position indexing throughout blocks, continuations, headings, HTML diagnostics and source-line bounds. It must not normalize away evidence offsets or certify phantom anchors.

**ROOT-R2-TABLE01 — Medium, confirmed, attributed to the coordinator; not my independent INITIAL finding.** The frozen parser has no table-cell/row boundary model before its code/comment/HTML/link-label interpretation. In a valid two-column GFM table, body row ``| `literal | [bad](missing.md) ` |`` wrongly lets backticks cross cells and hide the missing target. A matching backtick on a later table row likewise hides a real link. A cell ending in `<!--` masks a broken link in the next cell. A cell containing `<span id="fake|anchor"></span>` fabricates `fake|anchor` across an unescaped separator, allowing a later `#fake%7Canchor` fragment to pass. Conversely, `[bad | label](missing.md)` across cells is wrongly treated as a link and emits a false path finding.

I actually read GFM section 4.10 around Example 200, including per-cell inline parsing, escaped pipes, matching header/delimiter counts, table termination and excess body-cell handling. These justify bounded table context rather than making every pipe a global barrier. Same-cell escaped-pipe code and ordinary non-table pipe code must continue masking literal examples correctly. The independently executed controls above establish that distinction. [GFM tables extension](https://github.github.com/gfm/#tables-extension-).

Correction should recognize the supported table context before inline masks/extraction, prevent spans from crossing real cell/row boundaries, preserve escaped pipes and physical offsets, and retain non-table behavior. This does not require a complete GFM renderer, hosted checks or additional kernel testing. The defect is a supported-context composition error with tangible false greens and false positives.

The R1 single-line/title metadata checks and scalar-preservation corrections do not establish multiline block isolation, origin-aware URL splitting, consistent physical-line semantics or table-cell separation. Its final appendix does not establish that it saw the eventual 673-line R1 code final; the code final did read the 931-line security final. ### Unclosed R2-SF02 follow-up — Medium — CRLF continuation and blank-block boundaries

This same reviewer independently found these during frozen-fix verification; they are a follow-up to the peer's physical-line finding, not part of my INITIAL or a newly disclosed peer result. At [docs_consistency.py:342](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L342) and [343](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L343), `BLANK_LINE` and `SPACES` still recognize only LF patterns. The parsing view at 890 normalizes lone CR while deliberately retaining CRLF length, so the remaining CR characters are encountered by these LF-only matchers. Consumers include `close_label` at 417, `spaces` at 423 and `title_end` at 474. The [documented contract](../../../guides/ci.md#L143) promises consistent LF/CR/CRLF parsing and supported continuations.

With a real `target.txt` file, each source below uses actual CRLF bytes represented by escapes here:

```text
[x](\r\n target.txt)\r\n
[x](target.txt\r\n "title")\r\n
[x][r]\r\n\r\n[r]:\r\n target.txt\r\n
```

Both interpreters reject the first two as malformed/unclosed inline link syntax. The third yields undefined reference at line 1 and a missing-destination syntax finding at line 3. Every equivalent LF and CR control passes. This is a real false-positive compatibility failure, not unsupported grammar invented for review.

The companion source below is stronger because it silently hides a real broken link:

```text
[x](target.txt "first\r\n\r\n[real](missing.md)\r\nlast")\r\n
```

Both interpreters wrongly return exit 0 and no findings. LF and CR correctly diagnose the blank-line-separated title at line 1 and `missing.md` at line 3. A label `[first\r\n\r\nlast](missing.md)` also incorrectly becomes a cross-block link and emits a path finding, while LF/CR remain ordinary prose. No unescaped heading/fence interrupts these title fixtures; this is distinct from the author's correctly withdrawn exploratory title case. CommonMark's already-read LF/CR/CRLF and blank-line rules apply directly; no complete-renderer demand is added.

**Required action:** make continuation whitespace and blank-line boundaries honor the same physical-line model without shifting original offsets. Add all three endings, valid inline/reference continuations and invalid blank-separated titles/labels with exact source/target findings. Keep the already passing physical headings, `#L` bounds, HTML/error offsets and original-scalar controls. R2-SF02 cannot close while these cases fail.

### Unclosed ROOT-R2-TABLE01 follow-up — Medium — parent comment crosses erased table rows

This same reviewer independently found this during correction verification. It remains within the coordinator's table-context finding family; it is not retroactively an independent INITIAL discovery. At [table row erasure, 275](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L275) and [header erasure, 300](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L300), recognized table rows disappear from the parent parsing view. The subsequent [whole-parent comment pass at 899](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L899), implemented at [547](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L547), can still take a pre-table incomplete comment through those erased block boundaries to document end. Its closing marker was in a table row and is no longer available in that view.

Minimal complete `README.md` (no `missing.md` exists):

```markdown
prose <!--
| A | B |
| --- | --- |
| text | text |
-->

[real](missing.md)
```

On **6f9**, both interpreters correctly emit the line-7 path finding and exit 1. On **eb5**, both emit **zero findings and exit 0**. The independent before/after controls with an escaped opener, a real block-start comment, and an ordinary non-table inline comment all still emit the expected later real-link finding on both revisions/interpreters. A companion with a broken link inside the rendered table reports that line-4 target but misses line 7, demonstrating why checking only nonzero exit is inadequate.

I explicitly resolved the initial ambiguity before reporting a blocker: GFM table recognition can split a preceding paragraph, so the rendered table link is real and is not a false-positive comment literal. I actually read the official `row_from_string` and `try_opening_table_header` implementation, including paragraph splitting and per-cell nodes, in the [cmark-gfm table source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/github/cmark-gfm/master/extensions/table.c). The lookup returned lines 0–650; the relevant consumed regions are 177–281 and 282–393. I did not install/execute an external renderer or claim a full-library source audit.

**Required action:** enforce recognized block/table boundaries in parent comment/inline masking as well as child cell parsing, retaining original offsets and real block-comment behavior. A table-local repair must not leave pre-table inline state able to swallow later prose. Add the exact old-pass/new-fail regression and the escaped/block/non-table controls before correction. Do not exclude tables or broadly suppress malformed source. TABLE01 cannot close while this silent green remains.

The coordinator's 30 CRLF executions are attributed coordinator reproductions after my disclosure. I independently checked the inline-comment grammar against the actual primary [CommonMark 0.31.2 raw-HTML definition](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#raw-html), especially its returned line 8136: the two short forms are complete comments, and the ordinary opener requires its closing delimiter. An incomplete inline opener must not consume the later real navigation as if it were an indefinitely open block comment. Block-start comment handling remains a separate control.

No changing repository source was executed or certified.

### R2-SF02 family remains open — Medium — backslash skips a physical blank boundary

This is an independently discovered follow-up during actual f811 verification, not an alteration of either INITIAL or an attributed peer discovery. The previously disclosed CRLF whitespace cases now pass. However, physical-line equivalence still fails when a backslash immediately precedes the first newline of a blank-line separator.

At frozen f811, [close_label](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L432) unconditionally skips the following character at lines 432–434 before its blank-line check at 455–456. [title_end](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L507) does the same at 507–509 before checking 512–515. An LF, or the same-offset LF representing a lone CR, is consequently skipped. With CRLF only CR is skipped and LF remains visible to the newly repaired blank matcher. The problem is the ordering/meaning of escape scanning, not another missing CRLF regex spelling.

Create a README with these exact lines, a real `target.txt`, and absent `missing.md`:

```markdown
[x](target.txt "first\

[real](missing.md)
last")
```

On both interpreters, LF and lone-CR versions return **exit 0, one target, zero findings**. The actual missing target on physical line 3 disappears into a purported title. The CRLF version and each no-backslash control correctly report the declared line-1 `link-syntax` error (`Unclosed or blank-line-separated link title`) and line-3 `path` error (`Missing or case-mismatched local target: missing.md`). The corroborating source `[first\` followed by a blank line and `last](missing.md)` fabricates a cross-paragraph link for LF/CR; CRLF correctly leaves plain prose.

The actual primary [CommonMark link-title rule](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#links), returned at line 6769, excludes blank lines. Its [backslash-escape definition](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#backslash-escapes), returned at 377–396, permits punctuation escapes and treats other backslashes literally; block separation cannot be discarded by this scanner shortcut. This does not depend on the previously withdrawn interrupting-heading/fence title expectation. No external renderer was installed or executed.

The independent current matrix contains four cases × three endings × two interpreters = **24 executions**, with **eight mismatches** (two faulty forms × LF/CR × both interpreters) and sixteen matching controls, including escaped quote punctuation. All full sources/reports/exits/stderr were displayed and consumed. Eight additional runs against exact 6f9 and eb5 copies reproduce both LF failures on both interpreters, so this is a **retained defect, not a new f811 regression**. The clean suites/corpus do not dispose of it.

**Required action:** preserve physical blank boundaries through escape scanning. Distinguish the supported punctuation escapes from a backslash followed by line-ending syntax, consistently across relevant label/title/destination consumers; inspect shared consumers rather than adding an isolated spelling exception. Add exact positive escape/continuation controls and negative blank-title/label cases for LF, CR and CRLF before repair, retaining the real downstream missing target and full physical diagnostic oracle. Keep the previously passing source-kind, comment/table/container and original-data controls. No grammar exclusion, historical waiver or whole-renderer implementation is requested.

### Independent reproduction and reconciliation

**R2-SF03 — Medium, retained on eb5 and f811, independently reproduced after disclosure.** At frozen f811 `tools/docs_consistency.py:329–340`, replacing a block comment with spaces and then recomputing its block body manufactures indentation. Real HTML navigation on the comment's closing physical line is discarded as indented code. `<!-- c --> <a href="missing.md">real</a>` must report a README line-1 missing path; `<!--\n--> <img src="missing.md">` must report line 2. Both instead exit zero without a finding. The security reviewer's matrix covers LF, CR and CRLF on both retained revisions and both interpreters. I reran all records and agree with the finding and its retained-regression attribution. A real HTML `id` on the same closing line is likewise lost, causing a false missing-fragment diagnostic; this is demonstrated by my additional controls below, not attributed to my original INITIAL.

**R2-SF04 — Medium, introduced in f811 relative to eb5, independently reproduced after disclosure.** At frozen f811 `tools/docs_consistency.py:378` and `:417`, every syntactic list marker becomes a code/comment boundary. In `prose <!--\n2. <span id="ghost"></span> -->\n\n[x](#ghost)\n`, the non-1 ordered marker is soft continuation of the existing paragraph, so the complete inline comment must hide the span. f811 instead exposes the fake anchor and falsely succeeds; eb5 emits the expected line-4 `fragment` finding, `Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: ghost`. With `[literal](missing.md)` inside the same comment, f811 instead fabricates line-2 path navigation. The security matrix's ordered-1 interruption and next-line raw-HTML controls were also rerun. This is the security reviewer's independent finding, subsequently corroborated by this reviewer.

**Coordinator escape/destination/nonblank-content extensions — confirmed, not relabeled as my independent initial discoveries.** I reran all 84 disclosed coordinator cases against my retained f811 copy. All actual JSON, exit codes and stderr exactly matched the supplied capture: 76 semantic mismatches and eight controls. The new source proofs extend the retained escape/blank family to escaped physical newlines in angle/bare destinations, bare escaped ASCII space, and nine scalars that Python's broad whitespace predicates wrongly treat as Markdown blank content: vertical tab, form feed, U+001C–U+001E, U+0085, U+2028, U+2029 and U+00A0. Each scalar on the middle line of a complete inline comment wrongly exposes the literal link on line 3. Existing decoy files are intentional: accepting invalid escaped-newline/space destinations can otherwise be mistaken for a mere missing-path result. Lone-CR destination normalization also produces a wrong target identity in affected cases. The expected syntax failure, line identity and downstream real-link visibility matter separately.

The complete disclosed reproduction totals are **156 bounded CLI executions**, all matching the disclosed actual full JSON/exit/stderr: peer 72 (30 semantic matches, 42 mismatches) plus coordinator 84 (eight semantic matches, 76 mismatches). Matching the disclosed defective result is reproduction, not a product pass. The peer matrix contains six cases × three physical endings × two revisions × two interpreters; the coordinator matrix uses its supplied cases/endings on f811. All used own temporary fixtures and frozen copies with bytecode disabled, explicit own TMPDIR, empty child PATH and ten-second subprocess deadlines.

I additionally executed the ten supplied temporary tests-first methods separately with unittest fail-fast under `/usr/bin/python3` (actual 3.9.6) and `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.13` (actual 3.13.14): **20 commands, each exit 1 with one test / one failure**. ### Bounded read-only repair advice supplied to the sole author

Use one physical-line block-transition scanner as the owner of paragraph and container state, with original offsets preserved. Its event should include line start/end, body offset, quote depth, list stack (kind, delimiter, marker indent and content indent), paragraph identity and a transition reason. Distinguish an ordered list that interrupts an open paragraph from a later sibling of an existing list and from a list that begins after an actual blank or other block termination. Do not globally replace all ordered markers with only `1.`; the existing-list and blank controls above would regress. A soft `2.` must remain source content, not be stripped as a container prefix. Apply outdent/container closure before classifying a sibling, and retain continuation within the current item's content indentation.

Have code spans and inline comments consume these shared boundary events; they must not call `blocks()` or independently re-guess markers. Definitions, headings and block-body consumers should use the same final event/body offsets. If HTML-context discovery requires a preliminary code context, use the same bounded scanner with explicit inputs in two stages: preliminary original-line events, then final events that incorporate protected HTML spans. Keep each stage acyclic, with code-span indexing consuming events rather than discovering them. This is a design suggestion, not a proof that any implementation now has those properties. A multiline quoted HTML attribute containing marker/fence-like text is an explicit interaction to verify.

Preserve raw block-comment closing-line spans separately from Markdown masks. Continue their unchanged fragments only into HTML tag/navigation/anchor processing, through the original physical line end; do not reinterpret comment-padding spaces as code indentation or promote the trailing Markdown text into navigation. Raw HTML handling must bypass Markdown backtick/escape/title rules and retain multiline tag spans. Blank-line decisions must use the grammar's ASCII space/tab predicate on physical lines, not general Unicode `strip()` / `isspace()` results.

The primary [CommonMark 0.31.2 specification](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/) was actually consulted for physical-line/blank definitions (source lines 220–224), ordered markers and paragraph interruption (3707–3721), HTML block termination (2127–2136), and list-item indentation/siblings (4881–4964), with adjoining quote-laziness material also read. These rules support the specific oracles above; I do not claim to have run a complete CommonMark renderer or to demand one as Step11 scope.

### Confirmed R2-C02 — Medium: setext heading text crosses real block/container boundaries

**Status: requires correction, retained on f811 and 4573176; not introduced by this fifth increment.** This new independently discovered adjacent composition was sent only to the coordinator during the current independent pass. The coordinator confirmed it would remain undisclosed to the current peer until both completed checkpoints are preserved/read. It is not attributed to a peer or to my original INITIAL.

At frozen `tools/docs_consistency.py:1254–1257`, `_headings` walks backwards across any nonblank body that does not start with `#` or `|`. It consumes the final layout's body offsets but ignores the event boundaries and already consumed setext heading extents. This lets preceding list items, a preceding outside-quote paragraph or a completed prior setext heading become part of a later heading title. A real anchor is rejected and a concatenated phantom anchor is accepted, including exit zero for a link to the nonexistent anchor alone. The guide explicitly supports setext headings and container-aware source handling; this is within the current navigation contract, not a demand for a complete renderer.

Minimal phantom-anchor reproducer, placed alone in fixture `README.md`:

```markdown
1. First
2. Second
   ---

[x](#first-second)
```

Expected: exit 1, `README.md:5: fragment: Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: first-second`. Actual on both retained revisions and both interpreters: exit 0, no findings, one local target and one fragment. Replacing the destination with `#second` reverses the defect: expected success, actual line-5 `fragment` finding `Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: second`.

A second equivalent container composition is `First\n> Second\n> ---\n\n[x](#first-second)\n`. Only `Second` is the quoted heading; the outside paragraph cannot contribute. A third is `First\n---\nSecond\n---\n\n[x](#first-----second)\n`: two independent headings are expected, but the second title absorbs the earlier title and underline. The real `#second` is wrongly missing. Original physical lines remain accurate in these wrong results; the error is anchor identity, not line counting.

The [CommonMark setext definition and examples](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#setext-headings) require heading text to form its own paragraph, and Example96 explicitly shows consecutive setext headings without intervening blanks. The actual primary-source read included lines 1160–1164 and 1374–1403; list/container rules returned alongside the search were also consumed. This supports the concrete paragraph-boundary oracles; no renderer was installed or executed.

The **108-case confirmation** is nine cases × LF/CR/CRLF × old/current revisions × two interpreters: six real/phantom variants for list, quote and previous-setext boundaries all fail (72 mismatches); ordinary soft multiline heading, real blank separation and preceding ATX heading all pass (36 controls). All have exact expected full findings, exit and empty-stderr assertions. The initial sixteen exploratory outputs are retained separately and are not counted as additional unique defects.

**Requested correction:** make setext heading accumulation respect actual paragraph/container boundaries and previous completed heading extents while retaining legitimate multiline headings. ### Additional confirmed retained R2-SF04 follow-up — Medium: lazy inner quote dropped while outer list continues

**Location:** frozen `tools/docs_consistency.py:168–172`, with downstream shared-layout boundaries consumed by comments/code/anchors. **Attribution:** independently found during this CODE verification after the current peer checkpoint was withheld. It extends the previously disclosed SF04/shared-boundary family; it is not claimed as the security reviewer's new current finding or a new regression introduced by 457.

Minimal source, preserving physical newlines:

```markdown
- > prose <!--
  2. [literal](missing.md) -->
```

Expected no navigation findings: the omitted inner quote marker is a lazy continuation of the same paragraph within the list, and ordered `2.` cannot interrupt that paragraph. Current and f811 instead return exit 1 and the exact finding `{"path":"README.md","line":2,"code":"path","message":"Missing or case-mismatched local target: missing.md"}`. The same failure occurs with ordinary `continued` text instead of `2.` and with a code span instead of the complete inline comment.

The false-green variant is:

```markdown
- > prose <!--
  2. <span id="ghost"></span> -->

[x](#ghost)
```

Expected exit 1 and exactly `{"path":"README.md","line":4,"code":"fragment","message":"Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: ghost"}`. Actual current and f811 output is `{"checks":["links"],"ok":true,"files":1,"local_targets":1,"fragments":1,"findings":[]}`, exit 0 and empty stderr. The anchor exists only in a complete inline comment.

The [CommonMark block-quote laziness rule](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#block-quotes) permits omitted quote markers on paragraph continuation text. Its [nested-list examples and ordered interruption rule](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#list-items) support composition inside an existing list and distinguish `2.` from a paragraph-interrupting `1.`. I actually consumed the relevant rule text and examples, including lines 3316, 3382–3504, 3656–3688, 3716–3721 and 4390–4425. Applying those rules to these fixtures is an explicit reviewer inference; no renderer was installed or executed.

The implementation pops the inner quote when `continuation` fails, then succeeds at the outer list. Its special lazy-preservation branch tests `outdented_list` only; losing an inner quote with a still-continuing parent list therefore returns the shortened container stack. The layout manufactures a boundary through a valid multiline inline construct. This explains all four failing current forms (comment path, comment anchor, plain-text continuation, code span). The current implementation correctly handles the four controls: explicit quote markers, a real ordered `1.` interruption, a real blank boundary, and a top-level lazy quote. f811 also fails the explicit-quote/non1 and top-level/non1 controls, which the fifth fix genuinely repaired. The exact full-result matrix preserves this distinction.

**Requested correction:** preserve the complete active container/paragraph identity for a valid lazy continuation even when only an inner quote is omitted and an outer container still matches. Do not preserve it through actual blank, interrupting list/block, or nonparagraph transitions. Add tests first for all four failing forms plus the four controls, physical-line diagnostics, all endings and both interpreters; exercise downstream comment/code/anchor consumers of the shared layout. This is a correction to the existing selected grammar, not a demand for every CommonMark feature. Traceability: CR01/CR05/CR06/CR07, P4/P8/P11/P12, AC1/AC2/AC9 and DoD1/DoD2/DoD3.

### Attributed lazy setext variant — same open C02/SF04 boundary repair

Coordinator source:

```markdown
> prose <!--
===
[literal](missing.md) -->
```

Expected no findings: bare `===` with the quote marker omitted is literal lazy paragraph continuation, not an eligible underline and not a paragraph interruption. Actual old/current output reports `{"path":"README.md","line":3,"code":"path","message":"Missing or case-mismatched local target: missing.md"}`, exit1, empty stderr. Corresponding list, nested-list/quote comment and quote code-span cases fail identically. An explicitly prefixed `> ===` and a genuine blank control correctly expose the real line3 link. The original nested-lazy false-anchor witness remains open alongside these false-positive examples.

I actually read the primary [CommonMark setext rules and Examples92–96](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#setext-headings), including lines1336–1372 and the adjacent extent examples. Example93 explicitly keeps bare equals signs inside a lazy quote paragraph; Examples92/94 distinguish the outdented dash line as a thematic break. Applying those block rules to the inline comment/code fixtures is the stated reviewer inference. No renderer, browser DOM or complete CommonMark/GFM equivalence is claimed.

At current `paragraph_interrupt` around lines183–190, the unconditional underline-shaped check participates in the false boundary; `_headings` still has the separate backward-accumulation defect at1254–1257. The correction therefore must coordinate paragraph identity and eligible underline classification, not merely shorten heading text after navigation has already been exposed by a manufactured boundary.

### Bounded repair advice and criterion effect

1. Carry the actual active paragraph/container identity forward through the classified block stream. When only an inner quote marker is omitted and the line is valid lazy paragraph text, preserve the full active identity even if an outer list still matches. Real siblings, valid new blocks and actual ASCII blank lines end it.
2. Decide underline eligibility in that context. An eligible underline consumes the current paragraph in the matching container; a bare lazy `===` stays text and must not close code/comment context. An outdented dash thematic break remains a real interruption. Do not treat every raw equals/dash-shaped line as the same block event.
3. Accumulate only the current paragraph's visible source slices and original start line, then join/slug once when its valid underline is reached. Emit and clear the heading extent once so preceding completed headings, thematic breaks and other container paragraphs cannot join a later title. This avoids repeated backwards accumulation without requiring a richer speculative block model.
4. Preserve legitimate soft/multiline/lazy and inline-comment-masked continuation. A raw starts-with blacklist or indiscriminate blankness check over manufactured mask spaces can truncate a real paragraph. Use actual shared block/context decisions and the final relevant visibility, retaining source offsets and distinguishing source blank lines from masked content. Keep the supplied positive controls and duplicate-suffix/physical-ending checks beside the negative witnesses.

### Correctness — Medium SF04 follow-up: real outer-list siblings stay inside an old quote paragraph

At [block_body](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L154), especially the accumulated `outdented_quote`/`outdented_list` decision and final `container_parts` call around176, the presence of an omitted inner quote forces paragraph-only treatment even when an enclosing list item has also ended. This confuses a genuine sibling of that already-open outer list with a lazy continuation inside the quote.

Exact independently executed source:

```markdown
1. > prose <!--
2. [real](missing.md) -->
```

Expected `README.md:2 path: Missing or case-mismatched local target: missing.md`, exit1. Both457 ande018 return the full report `{checks:[links],ok:true,files:1,local_targets:0,fragments:0,findings:[]}`, exit0 and empty stderr. The same loss occurs with a cross-line backtick span, `)` ordered delimiters and two nested quotes. Replacing the real link with `<span id="real"></span>` then linking `#real` wrongly rejects that real anchor at line4. All variants reproduce under LF/CR/CRLF and both interpreters.

The unindented `2.` continues the existing outer ordered list; it is not the first item of a new list interrupting a paragraph. I checked the actual [CommonMark list/quote rules](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#list-items) and the primary [cmark block implementation](https://github.com/commonmark/cmark/blob/master/src/blocks.c): failed item continuation returns the matching LIST parent, accepts its next marker, and only uses lazy continuation if no new block was opened. Applying that rule to the complete-comment/code witnesses is the stated reviewer inference; no renderer was installed or executed.

**Action:** distinguish which actual container level remains matched and which existing parent list can accept a sibling before applying inner-quote laziness. Preserve the now-correct three-space-indented lazy-quote case and explicit quote soft-marker control. Plain-list siblings and blank-separated siblings already pass. Do not fix this by treating every non-1 marker as an interruption. This is a retained supported-grammar defect, not a newly introduced regression or an invitation to implement a whole renderer.

### Correctness — Medium C02 follow-up: heading masks manufacture visible whitespace and anchors

The original C02 extent/eligible-underline defects and bounded scan are corrected in their exact witnesses. A separate adjacent part of the supported heading contract still fails. [Comment masking](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L774), [heading input preparation](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1097) and [HTML/comment masking](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1250) replace removed markup with literal spaces. `_headings` then sends those mask bytes to `slug`, or strips and joins separate physical pieces at [line1322](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1322). Thus source-position placeholders become rendered heading characters, while multiline removed spans can destroy actual adjacency or spacing.

Exact independently executed false-green witness:

```markdown
# Alpha<!--x-->Beta

[x](#alpha--------beta)
```

The actual heading text is `AlphaBeta`. Expected line3 fragment finding for `alpha--------beta`; both457 ande018 instead return exit0, one target/one fragment and no findings. A separate `#alphabeta` probe is wrongly rejected at line3. `# Alpha<span>Beta</span>` behaves similarly: the fabricated `alpha------beta` is accepted and real `alphabeta` is rejected. Setext equivalents fail too. Ordinary two-space headings, genuine soft-line headings and HTML-looking inline code are passing controls.

```text
For `Alpha<!--
x
-->Beta
---

[x](#alpha-beta)
`, the multiline comment contributes no visible separator. Old457 correctly rejects this phantom at line6 (while still wrongly rejecting the real `alphabeta`); **e018 newly accepts the phantom**. With real spaces on both sides of that comment, the actual two-space slug `alpha--beta` is wrongly rejected. The new forward paragraph scan therefore repairs extent boundaries but does not yet preserve the heading's visible text through erased syntax. All full reports/physical lines, both revisions/interpreters and three endings are retained.
```

The primary [GitHub section-link rules](https://docs.github.com/en/get-started/writing-on-github/getting-started-with-writing-and-formatting-on-github/basic-writing-and-formatting-syntax#section-links) remove markup, retain its contents and map spaces to hyphens. The [CommonMark raw HTML rules](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#raw-html) distinguish a raw comment/tag token from surrounding text; newlines inside that token do not independently create visible soft breaks. These rules support the witnesses; I do not claim complete browser/renderer parity.

**Action:** retain original diagnostic offsets and actual paragraph extents while preserving a separate rendered-heading text view or equivalent erased-span provenance. Removed comment/tag bytes must contribute zero characters, genuine source spaces and soft breaks must retain their own meaning, and HTML-looking protected code must remain literal. Add real-anchor and phantom-anchor assertions together, including multiline comments, duplicates and all physical endings. Do not merely collapse whitespace globally, which would break the passing actual two-space control.

### Bounded repair advice and traceability

1. Keep visual indentation columns separate from source-character offsets. Calculate tab advancement from the actual current column, consume container indentation at four-column stops, and preserve any remaining indentation when a tab crosses the required content column. Avoid globally expanding inline text or manufacturing rendered spaces. Share the resulting container identity in preliminary and final paragraph events so comments, code spans, definitions and headings agree.
2. Resolve an actual sibling of an existing parent list before treating an unmatched inner quote as lazy continuation. Preserve the precise matched ancestor/item relationship; a single accumulated `outdented_quote` flag must not veto the outer-list sibling. Retain genuine indented/lazy quote continuations and the explicit quote/blank/soft-marker controls already captured.
3. Give heading rendering zero-width removal semantics for actual comment/HTML markup while retaining source-offset provenance for diagnostics. Preserve real outside spaces and real soft line breaks; do not convert masking placeholders into slug hyphens or globally collapse whitespace. Literal code and escaped HTML/comment-looking text must remain heading text. Reuse existing classified paragraph extents rather than rescan backwards or add a new renderer dependency.

These are constraints demonstrated by the exact source/control matrices, not a speculative requirement for additional architecture. The authoritative acceptance evidence will be tests-first failures followed by actual new committed-source verification.

### Confirmed remaining finding — C02, Medium: removing markup before emphasis interpretation changes the heading

**Location:** frozen [heading projection](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1375) and [ATX projection](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1391), passed into `slug` at [line1400](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1400), which runs `inline_text`/`plain_emphasis` after the intervening source has disappeared. Ignored spans correctly have zero rendered width, but their original delimiter-neighbor context is lost before emphasis is interpreted.

Minimal real-anchor reproduction in an isolated README:

```markdown
# _a_<span></span>_b_

[x](#ab)
```

Expected: no findings, exit0. Actual8ed: `README.md:3 fragment: Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: ab`, exit1. Replacing the actual tags with `<!--x-->` has the same result; setext forms diagnose the corresponding lookup on line4. These matched-emphasis/raw-HTML combinations are inside the stated heading grammar.

The paired phantom proof replaces the lookup with `[x](#a__b)`. Expected: the line3 (ATX) or line4 (setext) `fragment` finding `Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: a__b`, exit1. **Current8ed instead returns `ok:true`, files1/targets1/fragments1, no findings and exit0.** The original separated emphasis delimiters have been merged into `_a__b_`. This is an actual new false green compared with e018, which rejected the phantom. Rejection of real `#ab` is retained from e018, not a newly introduced regression.

The [CommonMark delimiter-flanking rules](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#emphasis-and-strong-emphasis) were actually read: source punctuation beside each underscore allows separate emphasis spans; HTML binds before emphasis ambiguity resolution. Applying those rules yields visible `ab`, consistent with the already reviewed [GitHub heading-link rules](https://docs.github.com/en/get-started/writing-on-github/getting-started-with-writing-and-formatting-on-github/basic-writing-and-formatting-syntax#section-links). This is a direct bounded grammar inference, not execution of an installed renderer. The new-delimiter list controls were separately reconciled with actual CommonMark §5.3/Example302; no parser dependency was installed.

|8ed |24 incorrectly rejected |24 incorrectly accepted |12 pass |48/60 |

This finding was independently generated during current verification and reported to root before any current peer disclosure.

**Action:** preserve original inline token/delimiter context through heading extraction, then emit zero-width markup removal. Do not join previously separated underscore runs before deciding emphasis. Retain the existing real spaces/soft breaks, source diagnostics and protected code/escape controls; reverting to whitespace masks or stripping all underscores would fail already captured controls. Add both real/phantom tag/comment ATX/setext tests before correction. Traceability: Correctness/Test coverage/Documentation/Integration roles; CR01/CR02/CR05/CR06/CR07/CR08; P4/P12; AC1/AC2/AC9 and DoD1/DoD3.
