## Correctness

**Blocking finding CODE-R2-REPL-C01 — Medium — mixed `*`/`_` precedence removes literal underscores and invents a heading anchor.** The current [plain_emphasis implementation](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L897) resolves only underscore delimiter runs. A competing star span can close before the underscore closer; that invalidates the intervening underscore opener. The underscore-only stack nevertheless removes the two underscores. [inline_text](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L956) and [slug](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1033) then produce an incorrect automatic anchor.

Minimal real-anchor fixture, in an otherwise empty temporary root's `README.md`:

```markdown
# *foo _bar* baz_

[x](#foo-_bar-baz_)
```

Expected complete report is `{"checks":["links"],"ok":true,"files":1,"local_targets":1,"fragments":1,"findings":[]}`, exit 0, empty stderr. Actual report has `ok:false` and exactly `{"path":"README.md","line":3,"code":"fragment","message":"Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: foo-_bar-baz_"}`, exit 1, empty stderr. Replacing the link with `#foo-bar-baz` should yield that phantom's fragment finding and exit 1; current source instead returns the clean complete report and exit 0.

[CommonMark 0.31.2 example 469](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#example-469) establishes the rendered text `foo _bar baz_`. Applying this project's documented lowercase/space/underscore-retention slug contract gives `foo-_bar-baz_`. This is ordinary emphasis composition within the [advertised heading grammar](../../../guides/ci.md#documentation-consistency), not a request for a complete renderer. The [GitHub section-link guidance](https://docs.github.com/en/get-started/writing-on-github/getting-started-with-writing-and-formatting-on-github/basic-writing-and-formatting-syntax#section-links) also describes removing formatting while retaining its contents.

I independently reproduced both real rejection and phantom acceptance for ATX/setext × LF/CR/CRLF × Python 3.9.6/3.13.14: **24 complete CLI observations**, all contradict their independently established expectations. Setext reports the real failure on physical line 4. Four additional parent/current real-anchor controls show this is a retained defect in the current supported grammar, not a regression introduced uniquely by the latest source-offset patch. Evidence: `mixed-emphasis-confirmation.json`, `mixed-emphasis-parent-control.json`, `spec-heading-probes.json` in my evidence directory below. No supplied author fixture was used as the oracle for this finding.

**Required correction:** resolve competing star and underscore spans in a shared delimiter context, or otherwise preserve underscores whose apparent pair is invalidated by the competing span. Add tests-first real/phantom regressions for this minimal case, both heading forms and physical endings, with neighboring valid nested emphasis and protected code/escape/link-label controls. Preserve source positions and the repaired HTML/comment/code whitespace behavior; do not globally remove underscores or add an exclusion. Re-run both suites, full-object controls and the public corpus, then obtain actual renewed fix verification.

The current correction otherwise matches its stated repair on the cases I examined and executed. Original contiguous source positions now drive underscore flanking and run adjacency; HTML/comments no longer merge runs or manufacture intraword neighbors. The source whitespace classifier correctly distinguishes CommonMark `Zs`/TAB/LF/FF/CR from Python's broader `isspace()` set. Matched code spans retain trailing spaces before a physical break; paragraph-leading indentation is removed before inline interpretation. Original ATX closers, zero-width markup, duplicate suffixes, nested containers and physical diagnostics remain intact on the full current suite and supplied exact replays.

The shared block/paragraph layout is a useful invariant: block extents, cell boundaries and original offsets feed code, comments, definitions and headings instead of independent backward reconstruction. Table cells preserve document-wide reference definitions while isolating inline state. Path handling distinguishes Markdown scalar decoding from HTML URL input cleanup, checks exact-case components and root/private containment, and rejects symlinked source inputs and nonregular reads. No additional correctness blocker was confirmed in those areas. Passing controls are bounded evidence; they do not cancel C01.

## Literal standard checklist

Each numbered prompt below is copied literally from the current code-review standard. Relative Markdown within the fenced prompts is literal checklist text, not report navigation.

### CR01

```text
**Correctness.** Does the code do what the description says? Are there off-by-one errors, uninitialized fields, missing error handling?
```

**HOLD, C01.** Full implementation/test/diff review and independent exact CLI probes confirm mixed delimiter precedence loses literal underscores; repaired HTML/comment/source whitespace/trailing-code semantics pass the supplied controls.

### CR02

```text
**Capability semantics.** If the change touches a trust boundary, is the capability check in the right place? Can an unauthorized caller observe or affect state they should not?
```

**Host analogue checked; no Tyrne capability delta.** Explicit root/corpus/private containment and source symlink/regular-file guards precede source opening; case/root/private alias probes and audit-hook authority controls pass. This is not a TOCTOU race proof or a process sandbox.

### CR03

```text
**Fault containment.** Can a userspace failure reach the kernel through this path? If so, is that intended?
```

**Kernel axis N/A; host analogue checked.** The command is a separate offline process and changes no kernel entry, IPC, syscall or runtime path; malformed/oversize/FIFO inputs produce findings/nonzero exits instead of entering Tyrne.

### CR04

```text
**`unsafe` discipline.** Any new `unsafe` must satisfy [unsafe-policy.md](unsafe-policy.md): `SAFETY:` comment, audit log entry, justification, reviewer signed off specifically on the `unsafe`.
```

**N/A to new unsafe.** No Rust/assembly/unsafe region or audit entry changed; quoted unsafe examples/history remain data. No kernel soundness claim or unsafe sign-off is made.

### CR05

```text
**Error handling.** Every `Result` propagated correctly? No new `unwrap`/`expect` on hot paths? Error types converted at boundaries (see [error-handling.md](error-handling.md))?
```

**Host analogue checked.** ValueError/OSError/RuntimeError/UnicodeDecodeError are converted at source/path/fragment boundaries, CLI argument errors exit2, validation errors exit1 and successful reports exit0. Exact error-path/line/FIFO/UTF-8/size/percent controls pass; native-interpreter failures and universal races remain outside this bounded evidence.

### CR06

```text
**Tests.** Do tests actually test the change? See [testing.md](testing.md) for what counts.
```

**HOLD for missing C01 regression.** Both actual140-method suites and694 exact replay cases pass; independently established crossing emphasis is absent from them. Fourteen before/stage reruns verify the retained failures/passes without inferring unreached cases.

### CR07

```text
**Documentation.** Every new public item documented? Every `unsafe fn` has a `# Safety` section? ADR references in commit messages?
```

**Covered with declared Step11 limits.** Complete current guide/task/plan/accounts and twelve material Markdown bodies reviewed. Usage/grammar/errors/limits and ADR trailers are documented. No new Rust public item or unsafe fn requires rustdoc/Safety; actual paired/hosted completion remains pending.

### CR08

```text
**Architectural principles.** Does the change respect [architectural-principles.md](architectural-principles.md), especially P1 (no ambient authority), P2 (small TCB), P3 (drivers in userspace), P7 (no proprietary blobs)?
```

**All P1–P12 individually assessed below.** Host equivalents cover input authority, bounded validation, source provenance and incremental delivery. No architectural-policy exception or new subsystem decision introduced.

### CR09

```text
**Dependencies.** Any new crate added? Has it been through the dependency-addition process in [infrastructure.md](infrastructure.md)?
```

**No dependency delta.** Python3.9+ standard library only, no Cargo/lock/toolchain/package change or install; existing installed interpreters used by absolute path. Dependency-addition process is not newly triggered.

### CR10

```text
**Naming and style.** Conforms to [code-style.md](code-style.md)? Clippy would already have caught most of this; reviewer does not re-litigate.
```

**No separate blocker.** Full Python style/organization review and partial existing-tool comparison completed, cumulative diff whitespace check clean. Rust Clippy is N/A; no fictitious Python formatter/linter was claimed. C01 records the concrete maintainability consequence of split delimiter state.

### CR11

```text
**Commit message.** Conforms to [commit-style.md](commit-style.md)? Trailers correct?
```

**Checked.** All15 cumulative commit descriptions/trailers read in chronological order. Current fix uses conventional fix(tooling) with Refs: ADR-0040, ADR-0043, T-056; no review/hosted/Done status is fabricated in the message.

## Architectural principles P1–P12

| Principle | Individual disposition |
|---|---|
| P1 — No ambient authority | Kernel capability semantics N/A; host caller explicitly selects root and receives findings. No credentials, network fetch, Git subprocess or repository write is required. Root/private/exact-case/source-alias probes pass; no OS capability sandbox is claimed. |
| P2 — Smallest defensible TCB | No privileged code changes; parser remains host tooling. Standard-library reuse and bounded inputs avoid adding a shipped/kernel TCB dependency; interpreter/native-code soundness not reviewed. |
| P3 — Drivers/filesystems/network in userspace | No driver or hardware placement delta. Host failures stay in the checker process; no syscall/IPC/kernel path is introduced. |
| P4 — Capability checks at trust boundaries | Kernel capability axis N/A; host analogue checks untrusted destinations, explicit corpus and containment before reads. Alias/FIFO/private/root/error cases pass; concurrent filesystem mutation remains an unproved limit. |
| P5 — Rust-only kernel code | Python is explicitly authorized host tooling under Accepted ADR0043; no kernel/HAL/service language or assembly/unsafe change. |
| P6 — HAL separation | No CPU, board, register or HAL integration changes; host path and grammar code does not enter kernel crates. |
| P7 — No proprietary blobs | No proprietary binary, firmware or new dependency added; complete script snapshots are inert JSON text, not installed executable packages. |
| P8 — ADRs for decisions | ADR0043 and the approved plan already settle language/corpus/incremental check policy. Current changes repair implementation semantics without overriding policy or creating new exemptions. |
| P9 — English repository | Source/prose/commit narratives are English; deliberate Unicode filenames/headings/fixtures test supported inputs. No repository content was authored by this reviewer. |
| P10 — Mermaid diagrams | No diagram added or changed; no raster/vector diagram substitution. N/A to parser logic. |
| P11 — Reproducibility | Exact HEAD/source/corpus/evidence hashes, both interpreter versions, full report/exit/stderr and cwd/root bindings independently checked. Four default-root corpus outputs identical; hosted Linux, native interpreter verification, binary reproducibility and universal resource guarantees not claimed. |
| P12 — No half-finished subsystems on main | Approved Step11 is explicitly incremental on development; Step12/13/task/hosted/maintainer gates remain open. C01 blocks current parser approval and no partial work is labeled task Done or main-ready. |

### Adopted peer blocker and coordinator extensions

I agree with **R2-RESUME-SEC01 — Medium**, independently discovered by the replacement SECURITY reviewer before disclosure. The minimal source is:

```markdown
# ` a `<!--x-->` b `

[x](#ab)
```

The two original code spans separately normalize their edge spaces, leaving visible `a` and `b`; zero-width comment content does not join their source delimiters. The real automatic anchor is `ab`. Current frozen7b44 and parent8ed reject it with the exact line3 fragment finding and exit1. Replacing the link with `#a--b` should fail but instead passes. This is a second concrete blocker mechanism alongside my independently discovered crossing-emphasis C01. It is not a native-memory, privilege, kernel or code-execution issue.

The source defect is at frozen [inline_text](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L956): original offsets are available but `CodeSpans(text)` indexes projected text. [Heading projection](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1374) removes zero-width markup and then reparses the newly adjacent characters. That can merge distinct original runs or manufacture other tokens. The existing original code-span records were used for trailing-space preservation but not the complete token identity. [CommonMark code-span semantics](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#code-spans) support the separate-span normalization premise; this is a source-based semantic inference and actual CLI verification, not execution of a GitHub renderer.

Root then supplied **root-attributed** adjacent composition cases. I read all six normalized complete fixture/full-report meanings in the72-row proof, encompassing twelve real/phantom anchor meanings. They are not blind independent discoveries by me. Source-token boundaries explain the five supported-grammar extensions below:

| Root family | Source content | Correct versus manufactured interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| Entity | `# Alpha &<!--x-->amp; Beta` | The comment interrupts the original character-reference spelling. Real `alpha-amp-beta`; manufactured decoded-entity phantom `alpha--beta`. |
| Inline link | `# Alpha [a]<!--x-->(target.md)` | The original source has no adjacent link destination after its bracket phrase. Literal destination text contributes to real `alpha-atargetmd`; manufactured-link phantom `alpha-a`. |
| Reference link | `# Alpha [a]<!--x-->[b]` | Two undefined standalone bracket phrases remain prose across the comment. Real `alpha-ab`; manufactured-reference phantom `alpha-a`. |
| Literal tag text | `# Alpha<sp<!--x-->an>Beta` | The interrupted tag spelling remains literal outside the actual comment. Real `alphaspanbeta`; manufactured HTML-tag phantom `alphabeta`. |
| Phantom HTML id | `<sp<!--x-->an id="ghost">` | The interrupted original tag cannot supply `id="ghost"`. The checker nevertheless accepts `#ghost` after masking changes the text presented to HTML interpretation. The expected line3 missing-anchor finding disappears. |

These are supported comment/entity/link/HTML/anchor compositions under the current guide, not a new broad grammar policy. The root literal-tag/id cases also show that masking a comment with whitespace can manufacture a valid tag/attribute interpretation, even where projected characters are not simply concatenated. A repair confined to merging backticks in heading slugs would therefore leave supported false greens behind. Original token validation must also govern the HTML navigation/anchor pass and entity/link/reference recognition. The [CommonMark raw HTML](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#raw-html) and [links](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#links) definitions support assessing the original syntax rather than newly assembled post-removal tokens. No blanket archive exception or acceptance of both real and phantom anchors repairs this family.

The sixth root fixture is **official CommonMark example481 autolink composition**, `# __a<https://foo.bar/?q=__>`, real `__ahttpsfoobarq__`, phantom `ahttpsfoobarq`. Its primary rendered-text oracle is correct, and its current mismatch matches my earlier exploratory observation. **Scope qualification remains:** the existing guide does not explicitly list autolinks among the selected forms. I do not retroactively convert my earlier scope-qualified probe into a blind independent discovery or a separately established-scope blocker. It is useful bounded adjacent precedence coverage for root's planned token-context correction; any new supported behavior should be described/tested with its exact scope. No complete CommonMark renderer is demanded.

## Correctness

**New confirmed blocker CODE-R2-REPL-C02 — Medium — URI autolink classification admits raw DEL and hides real navigation.** The [shared AUTOLINK regex](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L566) excludes `U+0000` through `U+0020` from a URI body but omits `U+007F`. The [explicit new guide contract](../../../guides/ci.md#documentation-consistency) excludes ASCII controls/spaces; [CommonMark's character class](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#characters-and-lines) includes DEL, and its [autolink grammar](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#autolinks) excludes that class. This is a concrete selected-boundary defect in the newly added lexical support, not a demand for complete rendering parity.

An exact reproducible source can be written as UTF-8 using this Python string (`\x7f` denotes an actual DEL byte, not four literal characters):

```python
source = "# <https://a/\x7f/_x_>\n\n[x](#httpsax)\n"
```

The source cannot form an autolink, so its ordinary matched `_x_` contributes `x`. Expected complete report is `{"checks":["links"],"ok":true,"files":1,"local_targets":1,"fragments":1,"findings":[]}`, exit0, empty stderr. Current source instead returns `ok:false` with exactly `{"path":"README.md","line":3,"code":"fragment","message":"Missing Markdown/HTML anchor: httpsax"}`, exit1, empty stderr. Replacing the link with `#httpsa_x_` should return that phantom's missing-anchor finding and exit1; current source returns the clean complete report and exit0. The invalid URI has incorrectly protected the underscores from ordinary inline parsing.

This also hides an actual file link, without relying on slug filtering:

```python
source = "<https://a/\x7f/[bad](missing.md)>\n"
```

Expected is `{"checks":["links"],"ok":false,"files":1,"local_targets":1,"fragments":0,"findings":[{"path":"README.md","line":1,"code":"path","message":"Missing or case-mismatched local target: missing.md"}]}`, exit1, empty stderr. Current source silently reports `ok:true`, zero local targets/fragments/findings and exit0. The old7b44 source checks that real Markdown link. An incorrect lexer classification therefore bypasses the intended navigation check; no external fetch, native-memory failure or kernel authority escalation is alleged.

Own actual confirmations are **24 current real/phantom heading runs** (ATX/setext × LF/CR/CRLF × both interpreters), **six current hidden-file-link runs** (three endings × both), **ten successful parent controls** (four heading, six file-link), and **four successful current positive controls** for percent-encoded `%7F` and non-ASCII `U+0085`. All complete JSON objects/counters/physical diagnostics/exit/stderr are retained. The30 current contradictions are one defect's observations, not30 unique findings. A separate45-case lexical boundary matrix on each interpreter finds only raw DEL misclassified among the independently declared control/scheme/domain limits.

**Required repair:** exclude raw DEL in the shared URI-body class so original-source parsing remains active for invalid autolink candidates. Add failing positive/negative contract fixtures first for the real anchor, phantom anchor and real embedded file link, with exact full reports on physical endings/both interpreters; retain valid encoded/non-ASCII bodies and valid URI/email lexical controls. Re-run retained suites/replays/corpus and meaningful adjacent checks, then obtain actual renewed verification. Do not broaden exclusions, accept both slugs, normalize away the source control, or treat this merely as a documentation omission.

**Previously disclosed blockers are verified repaired at this freeze.** My C01 mixed-star/underscore case now preserves the unmatched underscores and rejects its phantom. Adopted SECURITY SEC01 separate code spans retain their original token identity and per-span normalization. The five supported ROOT entity/link/reference/literal-tag/phantom-id forms now use original token boundaries rather than manufactured post-removal syntax. Exact current808 controls and146 methods pass; the relevant original before-stage failures are independently reproduced. These scoped closures do not approve current C02.

The new [InlineTokens](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L64) context explicitly carries original code/link/autolink extents. [inline_text](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L980) uses those extents, contiguous original positions for entity/escape recognition and protected emitted label/code/autolink text. [plain_emphasis](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L918) resolves competing star/underscore runs in one source-aware stack, dropping intervening delimiter openers when a competing match wins. [Original HTML checks](../../../../tools/docs_consistency.py#L1333) prevent comment masking from manufacturing tags and keep literal less-than from reaching HTML interpretation. Existing paragraph/container/table/ATX/duplicate/URL/entity/physical-line behavior remains successful on the retained and own focused controls.

The prior481 autolink observation is now within **explicitly added bounded URI/email support**, with fixtures and guide text under the existing fixture-first ADR0043 procedure. Its old7b44 contract-adjacent qualification is retained historically and is not rewritten into an earlier supported blocker or a new policy decision. Current481 controls pass. C02 concerns an explicitly documented boundary of this current addition.
